\paragraph{How to read this document.} Section~\ref{sec:func} treats the model mathematically, top-down: first the whole network as one composition $\Psi\circ\mathcal{B}^{(L)}\circ\cdots\circ\mathcal{B}^{(1)}\circ E$ of layers, then a definition of every layer down to single neurons and attention heads, then parameters, outputs, intermediate states and the local linear response. Section~\ref{sec:algo} gives readable pseudocode for the forward pass and for every distinct block type. Section~\ref{sec:compact} lists the weights of each block type \emph{once}, with how many times it repeats. Section~\ref{sec:gloss} explains in plain language what every kind of layer does. The original layer-by-layer table with every repetition written out is in Appendix~\ref{app:full}.

\section{Mathematical Representation}\label{sec:func}
We describe the model \emph{top-down}. First the whole network is written as one composition of functions (Section~\ref{sec:comp}). Then every function that appears in that composition is defined (Section~\ref{sec:layers}), down to individual neurons and attention heads. The remaining subsections describe the parameters, the outputs, the intermediate states, and the local linear behaviour.
\subsection{The model as a composition of layers}\label{sec:comp}
\begin{definition}[gemma-4-E2B as an $L$-layer decoder]
The model is a function $f_\theta:\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{Y}$ with parameters $\theta$. It maps a sequence of $T$ token ids $x\in\mathcal{X}=\{1,\dots,V\}^{T}$ to one logit vector per position, $y\in\mathcal{Y}=\mathbb{R}^{T\times V}$ ($V=262{,}144$). It is a composition of an embedding $E_{\mathrm{mm}}$, $L=35$ decoder blocks $\mathcal{B}^{(\ell)}$ and an output head $\Psi$:
\begin{equation}
  f_\theta \;=\; \Psi\circ\mathcal{B}^{(L)}\circ\mathcal{B}^{(L-1)}\circ\cdots\circ\mathcal{B}^{(2)}\circ\mathcal{B}^{(1)}\circ E_{\mathrm{mm}}
\end{equation}
Every block maps $\mathbb{R}^{T\times d}\to\mathbb{R}^{T\times d}$ ($d=1536$), so the blocks can be stacked in any number. Block $\ell$ has its own parameters $\theta_\ell$ and is stored in the checkpoint as \texttt{model.language\_model.layers.}$(\ell-1)$. Each block also receives its own per-layer embedding $p_\ell$ (Definition~\ref{def:embed}).
\end{definition}
\begin{definition}[Block types]
There are 4 block types (same function form within a type, different weights). A type map $c:\{1,\dots,L\}\to\{\mathrm{S1},\mathrm{F1},\mathrm{S2},\mathrm{F2}\}$ says which type layer $\ell$ is: $\mathcal{B}^{(\ell)}=\mathcal{B}_{c(\ell)}(\,\cdot\,;\theta_\ell)$. Here $c=((\mathrm{S1}^{4}\,\mathrm{F1})^{3}\,(\mathrm{S2}^{4}\,\mathrm{F2})^{4})$, read left to right from layer 1.
Written out, with the type as a subscript:
\begin{equation}
  f_\theta = \Psi\circ \mathcal{B}^{(35)}_{\mathrm{F2}}\circ \cdots\circ \mathcal{B}^{(5)}_{\mathrm{F1}}\circ \mathcal{B}^{(4)}_{\mathrm{S1}}\circ \mathcal{B}^{(3)}_{\mathrm{S1}}\circ \mathcal{B}^{(2)}_{\mathrm{S1}}\circ \mathcal{B}^{(1)}_{\mathrm{S1}}\circ E_{\mathrm{mm}}
\end{equation}
Grouping the repetitions with composition powers ($\mathcal{B}^{\circ n}$ = $n$ consecutive blocks of the same type, each with its own weights) gives the compact form
\begin{equation}\label{eq:compact}
  f_\theta = \Psi\circ\Big(\big(\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{F2}} \circ \mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{S2}}^{\circ 4}\big)^{\circ 4} \circ \big(\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{F1}} \circ \mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{S1}}^{\circ 4}\big)^{\circ 3}\Big)\circ E_{\mathrm{mm}}
\end{equation}
\end{definition}
\begin{definition}[Vision encoder]
The vision encoder is itself a composition of $16$ blocks acting on a sequence of image patches of width $768$:
\begin{equation}
  \op{Enc_V} = \op{Post}\circ \mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{V}}^{\circ 16}\circ\op{PatchEmbed}
\end{equation}
$\op{PatchEmbed}$ turns the raw input into vectors and $\op{Post}$ is the final norm / pooling / merging (Table~\ref{tab:nonrep}).
\end{definition}
\begin{definition}[Audio encoder]
The audio encoder is itself a composition of $12$ blocks acting on a sequence of audio frames of width $1024$:
\begin{equation}
  \op{Enc_A} = \op{Post}\circ \mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{A}}^{\circ 12}\circ\op{Subsample}
\end{equation}
$\op{Subsample}$ turns the raw input into vectors and $\op{Post}$ is the final norm / pooling / merging (Table~\ref{tab:nonrep}).
\end{definition}

\paragraph{Language-model decoder (35 layers).} 
layers 0--14: [$\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{S1}}$$\times4$, $\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{F1}}$] repeated 3$\times$; layers 15--34: [$\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{S2}}$$\times4$, $\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{F2}}$] repeated 4$\times$ (checkpoint indices, i.e.\ $\ell-1$).
\paragraph{Vision encoder (16 layers).} 
layers 0--15: [$\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{V}}$] repeated 16$\times$ (checkpoint indices, i.e.\ $\ell-1$).
\paragraph{Audio encoder (12 layers).} 
layers 0--11: [$\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{A}}$] repeated 12$\times$ (checkpoint indices, i.e.\ $\ell-1$).
\subsection{The layers, top to bottom}\label{sec:layers}
Every function in the composition above is defined here. Only the attention (or linear-attention) sub-layer mixes information \emph{between} positions $t$. Every other sub-layer (norms, feed-forward networks, projections) acts on each token vector $h_t\in\mathbb{R}^{d}$ independently, e.g.\ $\op{FFN}(h)_t=\op{FFN}(h_t)$. All weights follow the convention $W\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathrm{out}}\times d_{\mathrm{in}}}$, so a projection of a token vector is $W u$ (as in an affine layer $f_i(u)=A^{(i)}u+b^{(i)}$).
\begin{definition}[Token embedding]\label{def:embed}
$E:\{1,\dots,V\}^{T}\to\mathbb{R}^{T\times d}$ looks up one row of the embedding matrix $W_E\in\mathbb{R}^{V\times d}$ per token:
\begin{equation}
  E(x)_t = W_E[x_t,:]\cdot\sqrt{d},\qquad t=1,\dots,T.
\end{equation}
With images or audio, the multimodal embedding replaces the placeholder-token rows of $E(x)$ by projected encoder outputs:
\begin{equation}
  E_{\mathrm{mm}}(x,\cdot) = \op{Merge}\Big(E(x),\ \op{Proj}_{\mathrm{img}}\big(\op{Enc_V}(\mathrm{img})\big),\ \op{Proj}_{\mathrm{aud}}\big(\op{Enc_A}(\mathrm{aud})\big)\Big).
\end{equation}
Per-layer embeddings (PLE): a second table gives every token one small vector for each layer,
\begin{equation}
  p = \tfrac{1}{\sqrt2}\Big(\op{Embed}_{\mathrm{PLE}}(x) + \op{RMSNorm}\big(W_{\mathrm{ple}}\,E(x)/\sqrt{d}\big)\Big),\qquad p=(p_1,\dots,p_L),\ p_\ell\in\mathbb{R}^{T\times 256}.
\end{equation}
\end{definition}
\begin{definition}[Normalisation]
$\op{RMSNorm}(u)=\gamma\odot u/\sqrt{\tfrac1d\sum_i u_i^2+\epsilon}$, with a learned scale $\gamma\in\mathbb{R}^{d}$, applied to each token vector separately. It fixes the scale of a vector without changing its direction. Every norm in the model has its own $\gamma$, distinguished by a subscript (e.g.\ $\op{RMSNorm}_1$, $\op{RMSNorm}_{\mathrm{final}}$).
\end{definition}
\begin{definition}[Block $\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{S1}}$: Sliding-window attention block (MLP width 6144)]
A decoder block of this type maps $h\in\mathbb{R}^{T\times 1536}$ to the same space. It is a composition of 3 residual sub-layers, each of the form $(\mathrm{id}+g)(h)=h+g(h)$, scaled by the fixed per-layer scalar $\gamma_\ell$:
\begin{equation}
  \mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{S1}} = \gamma_\ell\cdot\big((\mathrm{id}+\op{PLE}_\ell)\circ(\mathrm{id}+\op{RMSNorm}_{\mathrm{pf}}\circ\op{FFN}\circ \op{RMSNorm}_{\mathrm{pre}})\circ(\mathrm{id}+\op{RMSNorm}_{\mathrm{pa}}\circ \op{Attn}\circ \op{RMSNorm}_{\mathrm{in}})\big)
\end{equation}
Step by step, the sub-layers are:
\begin{align*}
  a &= h + \op{RMSNorm}_{\mathrm{pa}}\big(\op{Attn}(\op{RMSNorm}_{\mathrm{in}}(h))\big) && \text{\footnotesize norm before AND after attention} \\
  b &= a + \op{RMSNorm}_{\mathrm{pf}}\big(\op{FFN}(\op{RMSNorm}_{\mathrm{pre}}(a))\big) && \text{\footnotesize norm before AND after the MLP} \\
  c &= b + \op{PLE}_\ell(b) && \text{\footnotesize inject this layer's 256-dim per-layer embedding $p_\ell$} \\
  \op{PLE}_\ell(u) &= \op{RMSNorm}_{\mathrm{ple}}\big(W^{\mathrm{ple}}_{\mathrm{proj}}(\op{GELU}_{\tanh}(W^{\mathrm{ple}}_{\mathrm{gate}}\,u)\odot p_\ell)\big) \\
  \mathcal{B}(h) &= \gamma_\ell\cdot c && \text{\footnotesize fixed per-layer scalar} \\
  Q &= W_Q\,u && \text{\footnotesize $H=8$ heads of size $d_h=256$} \\
  K,\ V &= W_K\,u,\quad W_V\,u && \text{\footnotesize $H_{kv}=1$ key/value heads} \\
  Q,\ K &= \op{RMSNorm}_q(Q),\ \op{RMSNorm}_k(K) && \text{\footnotesize per-head normalisation} \\
  V &= \op{RMSNorm}(V) && \text{\footnotesize no learned scale} \\
  Q,\ K &= \op{RoPE}(Q),\ \op{RoPE}(K) && \text{\footnotesize rotate by position} \\
  S_h &= s\, Q_h K_{g(h)}^{\top} + M && \text{\footnotesize mask $M$: causal + sliding window of $w=512$} \\
  P_h &= \op{softmax}(S_h) && \text{\footnotesize attention weights} \\
  O_h &= P_h\, V_{g(h)} \\
  \op{Attn}(u) &= W_O\,[O_1;\dots;O_H] \\
  \op{FFN}(u) &= W_{\mathrm{down}}\big(\op{GELU}_{\tanh}(W_{\mathrm{gate}}\,u)\odot W_{\mathrm{up}}\,u\big) && \text{\footnotesize gated MLP, width 6144}
\end{align*}
Parameters of one block ($|\theta_\ell|=36{,}184{,}064$): $W_Q\in\mathbb{R}^{2048\times1536}$, $W_K\in\mathbb{R}^{256\times1536}$, $W_V\in\mathbb{R}^{256\times1536}$, $W_O\in\mathbb{R}^{1536\times2048}$, $W_{\mathrm{gate}}\in\mathbb{R}^{6144\times1536}$, $W_{\mathrm{up}}\in\mathbb{R}^{6144\times1536}$, $W_{\mathrm{down}}\in\mathbb{R}^{1536\times6144}$, $W^{\mathrm{ple}}_{\mathrm{gate}}\in\mathbb{R}^{256\times1536}$, $W^{\mathrm{ple}}_{\mathrm{proj}}\in\mathbb{R}^{1536\times256}$, plus 8,192 parameters in norm scales, biases and other vectors. This type is used 12 times.
\end{definition}
\begin{definition}[Block $\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{F1}}$: Full (global) attention block (MLP width 6144)]
A decoder block of this type maps $h\in\mathbb{R}^{T\times 1536}$ to the same space. It is a composition of 3 residual sub-layers, each of the form $(\mathrm{id}+g)(h)=h+g(h)$, scaled by the fixed per-layer scalar $\gamma_\ell$:
\begin{equation}
  \mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{F1}} = \gamma_\ell\cdot\big((\mathrm{id}+\op{PLE}_\ell)\circ(\mathrm{id}+\op{RMSNorm}_{\mathrm{pf}}\circ\op{FFN}\circ \op{RMSNorm}_{\mathrm{pre}})\circ(\mathrm{id}+\op{RMSNorm}_{\mathrm{pa}}\circ \op{Attn}\circ \op{RMSNorm}_{\mathrm{in}})\big)
\end{equation}
Step by step, the sub-layers are:
\begin{align*}
  a &= h + \op{RMSNorm}_{\mathrm{pa}}\big(\op{Attn}(\op{RMSNorm}_{\mathrm{in}}(h))\big) && \text{\footnotesize norm before AND after attention} \\
  b &= a + \op{RMSNorm}_{\mathrm{pf}}\big(\op{FFN}(\op{RMSNorm}_{\mathrm{pre}}(a))\big) && \text{\footnotesize norm before AND after the MLP} \\
  c &= b + \op{PLE}_\ell(b) && \text{\footnotesize inject this layer's 256-dim per-layer embedding $p_\ell$} \\
  \op{PLE}_\ell(u) &= \op{RMSNorm}_{\mathrm{ple}}\big(W^{\mathrm{ple}}_{\mathrm{proj}}(\op{GELU}_{\tanh}(W^{\mathrm{ple}}_{\mathrm{gate}}\,u)\odot p_\ell)\big) \\
  \mathcal{B}(h) &= \gamma_\ell\cdot c && \text{\footnotesize fixed per-layer scalar} \\
  Q &= W_Q\,u && \text{\footnotesize $H=8$ heads of size $d_h=512$} \\
  K,\ V &= W_K\,u,\quad W_V\,u && \text{\footnotesize $H_{kv}=1$ key/value heads} \\
  Q,\ K &= \op{RMSNorm}_q(Q),\ \op{RMSNorm}_k(K) && \text{\footnotesize per-head normalisation} \\
  V &= \op{RMSNorm}(V) && \text{\footnotesize no learned scale} \\
  Q,\ K &= \op{RoPE}(Q),\ \op{RoPE}(K) && \text{\footnotesize rotate by position} \\
  S_h &= s\, Q_h K_{g(h)}^{\top} + M && \text{\footnotesize mask $M$: causal} \\
  P_h &= \op{softmax}(S_h) && \text{\footnotesize attention weights} \\
  O_h &= P_h\, V_{g(h)} \\
  \op{Attn}(u) &= W_O\,[O_1;\dots;O_H] \\
  \op{FFN}(u) &= W_{\mathrm{down}}\big(\op{GELU}_{\tanh}(W_{\mathrm{gate}}\,u)\odot W_{\mathrm{up}}\,u\big) && \text{\footnotesize gated MLP, width 6144}
\end{align*}
Parameters of one block ($|\theta_\ell|=43{,}262{,}464$): $W_Q\in\mathbb{R}^{4096\times1536}$, $W_K\in\mathbb{R}^{512\times1536}$, $W_V\in\mathbb{R}^{512\times1536}$, $W_O\in\mathbb{R}^{1536\times4096}$, $W_{\mathrm{gate}}\in\mathbb{R}^{6144\times1536}$, $W_{\mathrm{up}}\in\mathbb{R}^{6144\times1536}$, $W_{\mathrm{down}}\in\mathbb{R}^{1536\times6144}$, $W^{\mathrm{ple}}_{\mathrm{gate}}\in\mathbb{R}^{256\times1536}$, $W^{\mathrm{ple}}_{\mathrm{proj}}\in\mathbb{R}^{1536\times256}$, plus 8,704 parameters in norm scales, biases and other vectors. This type is used 3 times.
\end{definition}
\begin{definition}[Block $\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{S2}}$: Sliding-window attention block (KV-shared, MLP width 12288)]
A decoder block of this type maps $h\in\mathbb{R}^{T\times 1536}$ to the same space. It is a composition of 3 residual sub-layers, each of the form $(\mathrm{id}+g)(h)=h+g(h)$, scaled by the fixed per-layer scalar $\gamma_\ell$:
\begin{equation}
  \mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{S2}} = \gamma_\ell\cdot\big((\mathrm{id}+\op{PLE}_\ell)\circ(\mathrm{id}+\op{RMSNorm}_{\mathrm{pf}}\circ\op{FFN}\circ \op{RMSNorm}_{\mathrm{pre}})\circ(\mathrm{id}+\op{RMSNorm}_{\mathrm{pa}}\circ \op{Attn}\circ \op{RMSNorm}_{\mathrm{in}})\big)
\end{equation}
Step by step, the sub-layers are:
\begin{align*}
  a &= h + \op{RMSNorm}_{\mathrm{pa}}\big(\op{Attn}(\op{RMSNorm}_{\mathrm{in}}(h))\big) && \text{\footnotesize norm before AND after attention} \\
  b &= a + \op{RMSNorm}_{\mathrm{pf}}\big(\op{FFN}(\op{RMSNorm}_{\mathrm{pre}}(a))\big) && \text{\footnotesize norm before AND after the MLP} \\
  c &= b + \op{PLE}_\ell(b) && \text{\footnotesize inject this layer's 256-dim per-layer embedding $p_\ell$} \\
  \op{PLE}_\ell(u) &= \op{RMSNorm}_{\mathrm{ple}}\big(W^{\mathrm{ple}}_{\mathrm{proj}}(\op{GELU}_{\tanh}(W^{\mathrm{ple}}_{\mathrm{gate}}\,u)\odot p_\ell)\big) \\
  \mathcal{B}(h) &= \gamma_\ell\cdot c && \text{\footnotesize fixed per-layer scalar} \\
  Q &= W_Q\,u && \text{\footnotesize $H=8$ heads of size $d_h=256$} \\
  K,\,V &= K^{(\ell^*)},\,V^{(\ell^*)} && \text{\footnotesize from last non-shared layer $\ell^*$ (no K/V weights)} \\
  Q,\ K &= \op{RMSNorm}_q(Q),\ K && \text{\footnotesize per-head normalisation} \\
  Q,\ K &= \op{RoPE}(Q),\ \op{RoPE}(K) && \text{\footnotesize rotate by position} \\
  S_h &= s\, Q_h K_{g(h)}^{\top} + M && \text{\footnotesize mask $M$: causal + sliding window of $w=512$} \\
  P_h &= \op{softmax}(S_h) && \text{\footnotesize attention weights} \\
  O_h &= P_h\, V_{g(h)} \\
  \op{Attn}(u) &= W_O\,[O_1;\dots;O_H] \\
  \op{FFN}(u) &= W_{\mathrm{down}}\big(\op{GELU}_{\tanh}(W_{\mathrm{gate}}\,u)\odot W_{\mathrm{up}}\,u\big) && \text{\footnotesize gated MLP, width 12288}
\end{align*}
Parameters of one block ($|\theta_\ell|=63{,}708{,}928$): $W_Q\in\mathbb{R}^{2048\times1536}$, $W_O\in\mathbb{R}^{1536\times2048}$, $W_{\mathrm{gate}}\in\mathbb{R}^{12288\times1536}$, $W_{\mathrm{up}}\in\mathbb{R}^{12288\times1536}$, $W_{\mathrm{down}}\in\mathbb{R}^{1536\times12288}$, $W^{\mathrm{ple}}_{\mathrm{gate}}\in\mathbb{R}^{256\times1536}$, $W^{\mathrm{ple}}_{\mathrm{proj}}\in\mathbb{R}^{1536\times256}$, plus 7,936 parameters in norm scales, biases and other vectors. This type is used 16 times.
\end{definition}
\begin{definition}[Block $\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{F2}}$: Full (global) attention block (KV-shared, MLP width 12288)]
A decoder block of this type maps $h\in\mathbb{R}^{T\times 1536}$ to the same space. It is a composition of 3 residual sub-layers, each of the form $(\mathrm{id}+g)(h)=h+g(h)$, scaled by the fixed per-layer scalar $\gamma_\ell$:
\begin{equation}
  \mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{F2}} = \gamma_\ell\cdot\big((\mathrm{id}+\op{PLE}_\ell)\circ(\mathrm{id}+\op{RMSNorm}_{\mathrm{pf}}\circ\op{FFN}\circ \op{RMSNorm}_{\mathrm{pre}})\circ(\mathrm{id}+\op{RMSNorm}_{\mathrm{pa}}\circ \op{Attn}\circ \op{RMSNorm}_{\mathrm{in}})\big)
\end{equation}
Step by step, the sub-layers are:
\begin{align*}
  a &= h + \op{RMSNorm}_{\mathrm{pa}}\big(\op{Attn}(\op{RMSNorm}_{\mathrm{in}}(h))\big) && \text{\footnotesize norm before AND after attention} \\
  b &= a + \op{RMSNorm}_{\mathrm{pf}}\big(\op{FFN}(\op{RMSNorm}_{\mathrm{pre}}(a))\big) && \text{\footnotesize norm before AND after the MLP} \\
  c &= b + \op{PLE}_\ell(b) && \text{\footnotesize inject this layer's 256-dim per-layer embedding $p_\ell$} \\
  \op{PLE}_\ell(u) &= \op{RMSNorm}_{\mathrm{ple}}\big(W^{\mathrm{ple}}_{\mathrm{proj}}(\op{GELU}_{\tanh}(W^{\mathrm{ple}}_{\mathrm{gate}}\,u)\odot p_\ell)\big) \\
  \mathcal{B}(h) &= \gamma_\ell\cdot c && \text{\footnotesize fixed per-layer scalar} \\
  Q &= W_Q\,u && \text{\footnotesize $H=8$ heads of size $d_h=512$} \\
  K,\,V &= K^{(\ell^*)},\,V^{(\ell^*)} && \text{\footnotesize from last non-shared layer $\ell^*$ (no K/V weights)} \\
  Q,\ K &= \op{RMSNorm}_q(Q),\ K && \text{\footnotesize per-head normalisation} \\
  Q,\ K &= \op{RoPE}(Q),\ \op{RoPE}(K) && \text{\footnotesize rotate by position} \\
  S_h &= s\, Q_h K_{g(h)}^{\top} + M && \text{\footnotesize mask $M$: causal} \\
  P_h &= \op{softmax}(S_h) && \text{\footnotesize attention weights} \\
  O_h &= P_h\, V_{g(h)} \\
  \op{Attn}(u) &= W_O\,[O_1;\dots;O_H] \\
  \op{FFN}(u) &= W_{\mathrm{down}}\big(\op{GELU}_{\tanh}(W_{\mathrm{gate}}\,u)\odot W_{\mathrm{up}}\,u\big) && \text{\footnotesize gated MLP, width 12288}
\end{align*}
Parameters of one block ($|\theta_\ell|=70{,}000{,}640$): $W_Q\in\mathbb{R}^{4096\times1536}$, $W_O\in\mathbb{R}^{1536\times4096}$, $W_{\mathrm{gate}}\in\mathbb{R}^{12288\times1536}$, $W_{\mathrm{up}}\in\mathbb{R}^{12288\times1536}$, $W_{\mathrm{down}}\in\mathbb{R}^{1536\times12288}$, $W^{\mathrm{ple}}_{\mathrm{gate}}\in\mathbb{R}^{256\times1536}$, $W^{\mathrm{ple}}_{\mathrm{proj}}\in\mathbb{R}^{1536\times256}$, plus 8,192 parameters in norm scales, biases and other vectors. This type is used 4 times.
\end{definition}
\begin{definition}[Block $\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{V}}$: Vision encoder block]
A vision-encoder block of this type maps $h\in\mathbb{R}^{T\times 768}$ to the same space. It is a composition of 2 residual sub-layers, each of the form $(\mathrm{id}+g)(h)=h+g(h)$:
\begin{equation}
  \mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{V}} = (\mathrm{id}+\op{RMSNorm}_{\mathrm{pf}}\circ\op{FFN}\circ \op{RMSNorm}_{\mathrm{pre}})\circ(\mathrm{id}+\op{RMSNorm}_{\mathrm{pa}}\circ \op{Attn}\circ \op{RMSNorm}_{\mathrm{in}})
\end{equation}
Step by step, the sub-layers are:
\begin{align*}
  a &= h + \op{RMSNorm}_{\mathrm{pa}}\big(\op{Attn}(\op{RMSNorm}_{\mathrm{in}}(h))\big) && \text{\footnotesize norm before AND after attention} \\
  \mathcal{B}(h) &= a + \op{RMSNorm}_{\mathrm{pf}}\big(\op{FFN}(\op{RMSNorm}_{\mathrm{pre}}(a))\big) && \text{\footnotesize norm before AND after the MLP} \\
  Q &= W_Q\,u && \text{\footnotesize $H=12$ heads of size $d_h=64$} \\
  K,\ V &= W_K\,u,\quad W_V\,u && \text{\footnotesize $H_{kv}=12$ key/value heads} \\
  Q,\ K &= \op{RMSNorm}_q(Q),\ \op{RMSNorm}_k(K) && \text{\footnotesize per-head normalisation} \\
  V &= \op{RMSNorm}(V) && \text{\footnotesize no learned scale} \\
  Q,\ K &= \op{RoPE}_{2D}(Q),\ \op{RoPE}_{2D}(K) && \text{\footnotesize rotate by (row, column) patch position} \\
  S_h &= s\, Q_h K_h^{\top} + M && \text{\footnotesize mask $M$: bidirectional} \\
  P_h &= \op{softmax}(S_h) && \text{\footnotesize attention weights} \\
  O_h &= P_h\, V_h \\
  \op{Attn}(u) &= W_O\,[O_1;\dots;O_H] \\
  \op{FFN}(u) &= W_{\mathrm{down}}\big(\op{GELU}_{\tanh}(W_{\mathrm{gate}}\,u)\odot W_{\mathrm{up}}\,u\big) && \text{\footnotesize gated MLP, width 3072}
\end{align*}
Parameters of one block ($|\theta_\ell|=9{,}440{,}384$): $W_Q\in\mathbb{R}^{768\times768}$, $W_K\in\mathbb{R}^{768\times768}$, $W_V\in\mathbb{R}^{768\times768}$, $W_O\in\mathbb{R}^{768\times768}$, $W_{\mathrm{gate}}\in\mathbb{R}^{3072\times768}$, $W_{\mathrm{up}}\in\mathbb{R}^{3072\times768}$, $W_{\mathrm{down}}\in\mathbb{R}^{768\times3072}$, plus 3,200 parameters in norm scales, biases and other vectors. This type is used 16 times.
\end{definition}
\begin{definition}[Block $\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{A}}$: Conformer audio block]
A audio-encoder block of this type maps $h\in\mathbb{R}^{T\times 1024}$ to the same space. It is a composition of 4 residual sub-layers, each of the form $(\mathrm{id}+g)(h)=h+g(h)$, followed by a final norm:
\begin{equation}
  \mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{A}} = \op{RMSNorm}_{\mathrm{out}}\circ(\mathrm{id}+\tfrac12\op{FFN}_2)\circ(\mathrm{id}+\op{Conv})\circ(\mathrm{id}+\op{RMSNorm}_{\mathrm{post}}\circ\op{Attn}_{rel}\circ\op{RMSNorm}_{\mathrm{pre}})\circ(\mathrm{id}+\tfrac12\op{FFN}_1)
\end{equation}
Step by step, the sub-layers are:
\begin{align*}
  h_1 &= h + \tfrac12\,\op{FFN}_1(h) && \text{\footnotesize macaron half-step FFN} \\
  h_2 &= h_1 + \op{RMSNorm}_{\mathrm{post}}\big(\op{Attn}_{rel}(\op{RMSNorm}_{\mathrm{pre}}(h_1))\big) && \text{\footnotesize local attention, relative positions} \\
  h_3 &= h_2 + W_{\mathrm{end}}\,\op{SiLU}\big(\op{RMSNorm}(\op{DWConv}(\op{GLU}(W_{\mathrm{start}}\op{RMSNorm}(h_2))))\big) && \text{\footnotesize convolution module} \\
  h_4 &= h_3 + \tfrac12\,\op{FFN}_2(h_3) \\
  \mathcal{B}(h) &= \op{RMSNorm}_{\mathrm{out}}(h_4) \\
  \op{FFN}_i(u) &= \op{RMSNorm}\big(W_2\,\op{SiLU}(W_1\,\op{RMSNorm}(u))\big)
\end{align*}
Parameters of one block ($|\theta_\ell|=25{,}180{,}288$): $W_1^{[1]}\in\mathbb{R}^{4096\times1024}$, $W_2^{[1]}\in\mathbb{R}^{1024\times4096}$, $W_1^{[2]}\in\mathbb{R}^{4096\times1024}$, $W_2^{[2]}\in\mathbb{R}^{1024\times4096}$, $W_Q\in\mathbb{R}^{1024\times1024}$, $W_K\in\mathbb{R}^{1024\times1024}$, $W_V\in\mathbb{R}^{1024\times1024}$, $W_O\in\mathbb{R}^{1024\times1024}$, $W_{\mathrm{rel}}\in\mathbb{R}^{1024\times1024}$, $W_{\mathrm{start}}\in\mathbb{R}^{2048\times1024}$, $W_{\mathrm{end}}\in\mathbb{R}^{1024\times1024}$, $K_{\mathrm{dw}}\in\mathbb{R}^{1024\times1\times5}$, plus 9,344 parameters in norm scales, biases and other vectors. This type is used 12 times.
\end{definition}
\begin{definition}[Output head]
$\Psi:\mathbb{R}^{T\times d}\to\mathbb{R}^{T\times V}$ normalises the last hidden state and scores every vocabulary token, re-using the embedding matrix (tied weights):
\begin{equation}
  \Psi(h)_t = 30.0\cdot\tanh\!\big(W_E^{\top}\,\op{RMSNorm}_{\mathrm{final}}(h)_t/30.0\big)
\end{equation}
The $\tanh$ soft-cap keeps every logit in $(-30.0,30.0)$.
\end{definition}
\subsection{Neurons and attention heads}
\begin{definition}[Neuron, pre-activation value and state]
The feed-forward sub-layer of block $\ell$ contains $d_{ff}^{(\ell)}\in\{6144,12288\}$ neurons. Neuron $j$ of layer $\ell$ is the scalar function of a normalised token vector $u\in\mathbb{R}^{d}$
\begin{equation}
  \eta^{(\ell)}_j(u) = \op{GELU}_{\tanh}\big(W^{(\ell)}_{\mathrm{gate},j}\cdot u\big)\;\big(W^{(\ell)}_{\mathrm{up},j}\cdot u\big),
\end{equation}
where $W_{\mathrm{gate},j}$ is the $j$-th row of $W_{\mathrm{gate}}$. The feed-forward output is the sum of the neuron outputs, each written along its own column of $W_{\mathrm{down}}$:
\begin{equation}
  \op{FFN}_\ell(u) = \sum_{j=1}^{d_{ff}^{(\ell)}} \eta^{(\ell)}_j(u)\;W^{(\ell)}_{\mathrm{down}}[:,j].
\end{equation}
Across its $L$ blocks the network has $N=\sum_{\ell=1}^{L} d_{ff}^{(\ell)}=337{,}920$ feed-forward neurons. For input $x$ at position $t$, with $u^{(\ell)}_t(x)$ the normalised input of the feed-forward sub-layer:
\begin{itemize}
\item $\mathcal{V}(\eta;x,t)=W^{(\ell)}_{\mathrm{gate},j}\cdot u^{(\ell)}_t(x)\in\mathbb{R}$ is the \textbf{pre-activation value} of neuron $\eta$ (the argument of the non-linearity).
\item $\mathcal{S}(\eta;x,t)$ is its \textbf{activation state}: \emph{active} if $\mathcal{V}>0$ and \emph{inactive} if $\mathcal{V}\le 0$. The non-linearity is smooth, unlike ReLU, so an inactive neuron's output is strongly suppressed rather than exactly zero.
\end{itemize}
\end{definition}
\begin{definition}[Attention head and attention pattern]
Head $h$ of an attention layer $\ell$ reads from the whole sequence through its attention pattern $P^{(\ell)}_h(x)\in\mathbb{R}^{T\times T}$ (row $t$ = how much position $t$ attends to each earlier position) and writes through its own $d\times d_h$ slice $W^{(\ell)}_{O,h}$ of $W_O$:
\begin{equation}
  \op{Attn}_\ell(u) = \sum_{h=1}^{H} P^{(\ell)}_h\,V_{g(h)}\,W^{(\ell)\top}_{O,h}.
\end{equation}
$g(h)$ is the key/value head shared by query head $h$ (grouped-query attention). The pattern $P_h$ plays the role that the activation state plays for neurons: once $P_h$ is fixed, the head is linear in its values. The network has 35 attention layers $\times$ $H=8$ heads $=280$ attention heads.
\end{definition}
\subsection{Architecture and parameters}
\begin{definition}[Architecture and parameters]
The \textbf{architecture} is the tuple of sizes and the layer-type sequence:
\begin{equation}
  \mathcal{A} = (V,d,L,c,H,H_{kv},d_h,d_{ff}) = (262{,}144,\ 1536,\ 35,\ ((\mathrm{S1}^{4}\,\mathrm{F1})^{3}\,(\mathrm{S2}^{4}\,\mathrm{F2})^{4}),\ 8,\ 1,\ \{256,512\},\ \{6144,12288\}).
\end{equation}
The \textbf{parameters} are $\theta=\{W_E,\ \theta_1,\dots,\theta_L,\ \gamma_{\mathrm{final}},\ \theta_{\op{Enc_V}},\ \theta_{\op{Enc_A}},\ \theta_{\op{Proj}},\ \theta_{\mathrm{PLE}}\}$ ($W_E$ is shared with the output head). Grouping the blocks by type,
\begin{align*}
  |\theta| &= \sum_{\text{types } c} n_c\,|\theta_c| + |\theta_{\mathrm{rest}}| \\
  &= 12\times36{,}184{,}064 + 3\times43{,}262{,}464 + 16\times63{,}708{,}928 \\
  &\quad+ 4\times70{,}000{,}640 + 16\times9{,}440{,}384 + 12\times25{,}180{,}288 \\
  &\quad+ 2{,}787{,}746{,}336 \\
  &= 5{,}104{,}297{,}504,
\end{align*}
where $n_c$ is how often type $c$ occurs and $\theta_{\mathrm{rest}}$ holds everything outside the repeated stacks (embedding, final norm, encoder stems and projector, \dots; Table~\ref{tab:nonrep}).
\end{definition}
\subsection{Output representations}
\begin{definition}[Output representations]
For an input sequence $x$, the output can be read at three levels:
\begin{enumerate}
\item \textbf{Logits:} $y=f_\theta(x)\in\mathbb{R}^{T\times V}$, one score per vocabulary token at every position.
\item \textbf{Next-token distribution:} $p(x)_{t,k}=\dfrac{\exp(y_{t,k})}{\sum_{j=1}^{V}\exp(y_{t,j})}=\Pr\big(x_{t+1}=k\mid x_{1},\dots,x_{t}\big)$.
\item \textbf{Predicted next token:} $z(x)=\operatorname{argmax}_{k\in\{1,\dots,V\}} y_{T,k}$ (greedy decoding). Generation appends $z(x)$ (or a sample from $p(x)_{T}$) to $x$ and applies $f_\theta$ again; in practice the keys and values of earlier positions are cached.
\end{enumerate}
The model is \textbf{causal}: $y_t$ depends only on $x_1,\dots,x_t$, because every attention mask hides later positions (sliding-window layers additionally hide positions more than $w$ tokens back). Training minimises the cross-entropy $-\sum_{t}\log p(x)_{t,x_{t+1}}$.
\end{definition}
\subsection{Intermediate states and sub-network decomposition}
To study what happens at one layer $\ell\in\{1,\dots,L\}$, split the composition into a prefix, the layer itself and a suffix:
\begin{equation}
  f_\theta = \underbrace{\Psi\circ\mathcal{B}^{(L)}\circ\cdots\circ\mathcal{B}^{(\ell+1)}}_{G_{\ell+1}\ \text{(suffix)}}\circ\underbrace{\mathcal{B}^{(\ell)}}_{\text{layer }\ell}\circ\underbrace{\mathcal{B}^{(\ell-1)}\circ\cdots\circ\mathcal{B}^{(1)}\circ E_{\mathrm{mm}}}_{F_{\ell-1}\ \text{(prefix)}}
\end{equation}
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{Prefix} $F_{\ell-1}:\mathcal{X}\to\mathbb{R}^{T\times d}$ maps the tokens to the hidden state entering layer $\ell$ ($F_0=E_{\mathrm{mm}}$).
\item \textbf{Hidden state} (residual stream) after layer $\ell$: $h^{(\ell)}=F_\ell(x)=\mathcal{B}^{(\ell)}(h^{(\ell-1)},\,p_\ell)\in\mathbb{R}^{T\times d}$, with $h^{(0)}=E_{\mathrm{mm}}(x)$.
\item \textbf{Inside layer $\ell$:} the attention write $a^{(\ell)}=\op{RMSNorm}_{\mathrm{pa}}\big(\op{Attn}_\ell(\op{RMSNorm}_{\mathrm{in}}(h^{(\ell-1)}))\big)$, the mid-block state $\hat h^{(\ell)}=h^{(\ell-1)}+a^{(\ell)}$, the feed-forward input $u^{(\ell)}=\op{RMSNorm}_{\mathrm{pre}}(\hat h^{(\ell)})$, the pre-activations $\hat x^{(\ell)}=u^{(\ell)}W^{(\ell)\top}_{\mathrm{gate}}\in\mathbb{R}^{T\times d_{ff}}$ and the neuron outputs $\eta^{(\ell)}(u^{(\ell)})\in\mathbb{R}^{T\times d_{ff}}$, and the feed-forward write $m^{(\ell)}=\op{RMSNorm}_{\mathrm{pf}}\big(\op{FFN}_\ell(u^{(\ell)})\big)$.
\item \textbf{Suffix} $G_{\ell+1}:\mathbb{R}^{T\times d}\to\mathbb{R}^{T\times V}$ maps $h^{(\ell)}$ to the logits ($G_{L+1}=\Psi$).
\end{itemize}
\paragraph{Residual stream.} Because every sub-layer only \emph{adds} to the hidden state, unrolling the composition gives the final hidden state as the embedding plus everything the layers wrote:
\begin{equation}
  h^{(L)} = \Big(\prod_{k=1}^{L}\gamma_k\Big)\,h^{(0)} + \sum_{\ell=1}^{L}\Big(\prod_{k=\ell}^{L}\gamma_k\Big)\big(a^{(\ell)}+m^{(\ell)}+c^{(\ell)}\big)
\end{equation}
with $c^{(\ell)}=\op{PLE}_\ell(\hat h^{(\ell)}+m^{(\ell)})$ the per-layer-embedding write.
The fixed scalars $\gamma_\ell$ rescale everything written so far, so earlier writes are multiplied by all later scalars.
So $f_\theta(x)=\Psi(h^{(L)})$: each layer reads the current stream and adds a correction to it.
\subsection{Local linear representation}
A ReLU network is piecewise affine: inside a polytope cell it collapses to one matrix. This model is not, because softmax, $\op{GELU}_{\tanh}$ and the normalisations are smooth. Its local behaviour is described by the first-order (Jacobian) linearisation instead. Tokens are discrete, so we perturb the embedded input $h^{(0)}$.
\begin{definition}[Local linear response]
Let $J_\ell=D\mathcal{B}^{(\ell)}(h^{(\ell-1)})$ be the Jacobian of block $\ell$ at the current hidden state (tensors flattened to vectors). The chain rule applied to the residual sub-layers $(\mathrm{id}+g_{\ell,i})$ gives
\begin{equation}
  J_\ell = \gamma_\ell\,(I+Dg_{\ell,3})(I+Dg_{\ell,2})(I+Dg_{\ell,1}),
\end{equation}
where $g_{\ell,1}$ is the attention branch, $g_{\ell,2}$ the feed-forward branch, $g_{\ell,3}$ the PLE branch and each $Dg$ is taken at that branch's input. For a small perturbation $\delta$ of $h^{(0)}$, the response of the network decomposed around layer $\ell$ is
\begin{equation}
  f_\theta(h^{(0)}+\delta)-f_\theta(h^{(0)}) \approx G^{(\ell+1)}_x\cdot J_\ell\cdot F^{(\ell-1)}_x\cdot\delta,
\end{equation}
with $F^{(\ell-1)}_x=J_{\ell-1}\cdots J_1$ (prefix) and $G^{(\ell+1)}_x=J_\Psi\,J_L\cdots J_{\ell+1}$ (suffix). Multiplying out the products of $(I+Dg)$ terms writes $J_\Psi J_L\cdots J_1$ as a sum over \emph{paths}: the identity path straight from embedding to head, plus one term for every subset of sub-layers a signal passes through.
\end{definition}
\subsection{Summary of notation}
\begin{longtable}{l>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{10.5cm}}
\toprule
Symbol & Meaning \\
\midrule
\endfirsthead
\toprule
Symbol & Meaning \\
\midrule
\endhead
\midrule \multicolumn{2}{r}{\textit{continued on next page}} \\
\endfoot
\bottomrule
\endlastfoot
$f_\theta:\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{Y}$ & the whole model, $\{1..V\}^T\to\mathbb{R}^{T\times V}$ \\
$E_{\mathrm{mm}},\ \Psi$ & embedding and output head \\
$\op{Enc_V},\ \op{Enc_A},\ \op{Proj}$ & vision / audio encoders and their projectors into the text width \\
$\mathcal{B}^{(\ell)},\ \theta_\ell$ & block $\ell$ and its parameters, $\ell=1,\dots,L$ \\
$p_\ell$ & per-layer embedding fed to block $\ell$ \\
$\mathcal{B}_c,\ c(\ell)$ & block type $c$; type of layer $\ell$ \\
$\mathcal{B}^{\circ n}$ & $n$ consecutive blocks of one type (each with its own weights) \\
$(\mathrm{id}+g)$ & residual sub-layer $h\mapsto h+g(h)$ \\
$h^{(\ell)}$ & hidden state (residual stream) after layer $\ell$; $h^{(0)}$ = embeddings \\
$a^{(\ell)},\ m^{(\ell)}$ & what the attention / feed-forward sub-layer of layer $\ell$ adds to the stream \\
$\eta^{(\ell)}_j$ & $j$-th feed-forward neuron of layer $\ell$ \\
$\mathcal{V}(\eta;x,t),\ \mathcal{S}(\eta;x,t)$ & pre-activation value and active/inactive state of a neuron \\
$P^{(\ell)}_h$ & attention pattern of head $h$ in layer $\ell$ ($T\times T$) \\
$F_{\ell-1},\ G_{\ell+1}$ & prefix (embedding to layer $\ell-1$) and suffix (layer $\ell+1$ to logits) \\
$J_\ell,\ F^{(\ell-1)}_x,\ G^{(\ell+1)}_x$ & Jacobian of block $\ell$, and of the prefix / suffix at input $x$ \\
$p(x),\ z(x)$ & next-token distribution and greedy next-token prediction \\
\end{longtable}
\section{Forward Pass in Pseudocode}\label{sec:algo}
\begin{algorithm}[H]
\caption{gemma-4-E2B: full forward pass}
\begin{algorithmic}[1]
\Require token ids $x$ of shape $B\times T$; optional images / audio
\State $h \gets E(x)\cdot\sqrt{d}$ \Comment{$h^{(0)}$: one vector per token, $B{\times}T{\times}1536$}
\If{image input present}
\State $e \gets \op{Enc_V}(\text{image})$ \Comment{vision encoder: 16 layers, Algorithm~\ref{alg:vision}}
\State $h[\text{image token slots}] \gets \op{Proj}(e)$ \Comment{project into text width 1536}
\EndIf
\If{audio input present}
\State $e \gets \op{Enc_A}(\text{audio})$ \Comment{audio encoder: 12 layers, Algorithm~\ref{alg:audio}}
\State $h[\text{audio token slots}] \gets \op{Proj}(e)$ \Comment{project into text width 1536}
\EndIf
\State $p \gets \op{PerLayerInputs}(x, h)$ \Comment{35 small vectors per token, one for each layer}
\State $\ell \gets 0$
\For{$r \gets 1$ \textbf{to} $3$} \Comment{layers 1--15: pattern of 5 blocks, repeated 3 times}
\For{$j \gets 1$ \textbf{to} $4$}
\State $\ell \gets \ell+1;\ h \gets \mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{S1}}(h,\,p_\ell;\,\theta_\ell)$ \Comment{Sliding-window attention block (MLP width 6144)}
\EndFor
\State $\ell \gets \ell+1;\ h \gets \mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{F1}}(h,\,p_\ell;\,\theta_\ell)$ \Comment{Full (global) attention block (MLP width 6144)}
\EndFor
\State $\ell \gets 15$
\For{$r \gets 1$ \textbf{to} $4$} \Comment{layers 16--35: pattern of 5 blocks, repeated 4 times}
\For{$j \gets 1$ \textbf{to} $4$}
\State $\ell \gets \ell+1;\ h \gets \mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{S2}}(h,\,p_\ell;\,\theta_\ell)$ \Comment{Sliding-window attention block (KV-shared, MLP width 12288)}
\EndFor
\State $\ell \gets \ell+1;\ h \gets \mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{F2}}(h,\,p_\ell;\,\theta_\ell)$ \Comment{Full (global) attention block (KV-shared, MLP width 12288)}
\EndFor
\State $h \gets \op{RMSNorm}_{\mathrm{final}}(h)$ \Comment{$h=h^{(L)}$; this and the next step are $\Psi$}
\State $y \gets h\,W_E^{\top}$ \Comment{logits $B{\times}T{\times}262,144$, embedding matrix re-used}
\State $y \gets 30.0\tanh(y/30.0)$ \Comment{soft-cap logits}
\State \Return $y$ \Comment{softmax$(y)$ = next-token probabilities}
\end{algorithmic}
\end{algorithm}
\begin{algorithm}[H]
\caption{Vision encoder}\label{alg:vision}
\begin{algorithmic}[1]
\State $h \gets \op{PatchEmbed}(\text{pixels}) + \text{position embedding}$ \Comment{one vector per image patch}
\For{$\ell \gets 1$ \textbf{to} $16$} \Comment{16 blocks of type $\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{V}}$}
\State $h \gets \mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{V}}(h;\,\theta_\ell)$ \Comment{Vision encoder block; result is $h^{(\ell)}$}
\EndFor
\State $h \gets \op{Post}(h)$ \Comment{final norm / pooling / merging, see Table~\ref{tab:nonrep}}
\State \Return $h$
\end{algorithmic}
\end{algorithm}
\begin{algorithm}[H]
\caption{Audio encoder}\label{alg:audio}
\begin{algorithmic}[1]
\State $h \gets \op{Subsample}(\text{mel spectrogram})$ \Comment{2 strided convs: 4$\times$ fewer frames}
\For{$\ell \gets 1$ \textbf{to} $12$} \Comment{12 blocks of type $\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{A}}$}
\State $h \gets \mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{A}}(h;\,\theta_\ell)$ \Comment{Conformer audio block; result is $h^{(\ell)}$}
\EndFor
\State $h \gets \op{Post}(h)$ \Comment{final norm / pooling / merging, see Table~\ref{tab:nonrep}}
\State \Return $h$
\end{algorithmic}
\end{algorithm}
\begin{algorithm}[H]
\caption{Block $\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{S1}}(h)$ --- Sliding-window attention block (MLP width 6144)}
\begin{algorithmic}[1]
\Require $h$ of shape $B\times T\times 1536$, per-layer input $p_\ell$
\State $r \gets h$ \Comment{keep the input for the residual (skip) connection}
\State $u \gets \op{RMSNorm}(h)$ \Comment{normalise every token vector}
\Statex \hspace{-1.2em}\textit{token mixing: multi-head self-attention}
\State $Q \gets u W_Q^{\top}$ \Comment{$B{\times}T{\times}8{\times}256$ (8 query heads)}
\State $K \gets u W_K^{\top};\ V \gets u W_V^{\top}$ \Comment{$B{\times}T{\times}1{\times}256$ (1 key/value heads, each shared by 8 query heads)}
\State $Q \gets \op{RMSNorm}(Q);\ K \gets \op{RMSNorm}(K)$ \Comment{normalise each head vector (stabilises training)}
\State $V \gets \op{RMSNorm}(V)$
\State $Q, K \gets \op{RoPE}(Q, K)$ \Comment{encode position by rotating vector pairs}
\State $P \gets \op{softmax}(s\, Q K^{\top} + M)$ \Comment{mask: causal + only the last 512 tokens}
\State $O \gets P V$ \Comment{$B{\times}T{\times}8{\times}256$}
\State $m \gets O\, W_O^{\top}$ \Comment{concatenate heads, project back to $B{\times}T{\times}1536$}
\State $h \gets r + \op{RMSNorm}(m)$ \Comment{post-attention norm, then add residual}
\Statex \hspace{-1.2em}\textit{channel mixing: feed-forward network (each token independently)}
\State $r \gets h;\ u \gets \op{RMSNorm}(h)$
\State $m \gets \big(\op{GELU}_{\tanh}(u W_{\mathrm{gate}}^{\top}) \odot u W_{\mathrm{up}}^{\top}\big) W_{\mathrm{down}}^{\top}$ \Comment{expand to 6144, gate, shrink back to 1536}
\State $h \gets r + \op{RMSNorm}(m)$ \Comment{post-feed-forward norm, then add residual}
\Statex \hspace{-1.2em}\textit{per-layer embedding injection}
\State $h \gets h + \op{RMSNorm}\big((\op{GELU}_{\tanh}(h W^{\mathrm{ple}\top}_{\mathrm{gate}}) \odot p_\ell) W^{\mathrm{ple}\top}_{\mathrm{proj}}\big)$ \Comment{gate down to 256, multiply by $p_\ell$, project back up}
\State $h \gets \gamma_\ell\, h$ \Comment{fixed per-layer scale}
\State \Return $h$
\end{algorithmic}
\end{algorithm}
\begin{algorithm}[H]
\caption{Block $\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{F1}}(h)$ --- Full (global) attention block (MLP width 6144)}
\begin{algorithmic}[1]
\Require $h$ of shape $B\times T\times 1536$, per-layer input $p_\ell$
\State $r \gets h$ \Comment{keep the input for the residual (skip) connection}
\State $u \gets \op{RMSNorm}(h)$ \Comment{normalise every token vector}
\Statex \hspace{-1.2em}\textit{token mixing: multi-head self-attention}
\State $Q \gets u W_Q^{\top}$ \Comment{$B{\times}T{\times}8{\times}512$ (8 query heads)}
\State $K \gets u W_K^{\top};\ V \gets u W_V^{\top}$ \Comment{$B{\times}T{\times}1{\times}512$ (1 key/value heads, each shared by 8 query heads)}
\State $Q \gets \op{RMSNorm}(Q);\ K \gets \op{RMSNorm}(K)$ \Comment{normalise each head vector (stabilises training)}
\State $V \gets \op{RMSNorm}(V)$
\State $Q, K \gets \op{RoPE}(Q, K)$ \Comment{encode position by rotating vector pairs}
\State $P \gets \op{softmax}(s\, Q K^{\top} + M)$ \Comment{mask: causal: a token sees only earlier tokens}
\State $O \gets P V$ \Comment{$B{\times}T{\times}8{\times}512$}
\State $m \gets O\, W_O^{\top}$ \Comment{concatenate heads, project back to $B{\times}T{\times}1536$}
\State $h \gets r + \op{RMSNorm}(m)$ \Comment{post-attention norm, then add residual}
\Statex \hspace{-1.2em}\textit{channel mixing: feed-forward network (each token independently)}
\State $r \gets h;\ u \gets \op{RMSNorm}(h)$
\State $m \gets \big(\op{GELU}_{\tanh}(u W_{\mathrm{gate}}^{\top}) \odot u W_{\mathrm{up}}^{\top}\big) W_{\mathrm{down}}^{\top}$ \Comment{expand to 6144, gate, shrink back to 1536}
\State $h \gets r + \op{RMSNorm}(m)$ \Comment{post-feed-forward norm, then add residual}
\Statex \hspace{-1.2em}\textit{per-layer embedding injection}
\State $h \gets h + \op{RMSNorm}\big((\op{GELU}_{\tanh}(h W^{\mathrm{ple}\top}_{\mathrm{gate}}) \odot p_\ell) W^{\mathrm{ple}\top}_{\mathrm{proj}}\big)$ \Comment{gate down to 256, multiply by $p_\ell$, project back up}
\State $h \gets \gamma_\ell\, h$ \Comment{fixed per-layer scale}
\State \Return $h$
\end{algorithmic}
\end{algorithm}
\begin{algorithm}[H]
\caption{Block $\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{S2}}(h)$ --- Sliding-window attention block (KV-shared, MLP width 12288)}
\begin{algorithmic}[1]
\Require $h$ of shape $B\times T\times 1536$, per-layer input $p_\ell$
\State $r \gets h$ \Comment{keep the input for the residual (skip) connection}
\State $u \gets \op{RMSNorm}(h)$ \Comment{normalise every token vector}
\Statex \hspace{-1.2em}\textit{token mixing: multi-head self-attention}
\State $Q \gets u W_Q^{\top}$ \Comment{$B{\times}T{\times}8{\times}256$ (8 query heads)}
\State $K, V \gets K^{(\ell^*)}, V^{(\ell^*)}$ \Comment{re-use keys/values of an earlier layer (saves compute and KV-cache)}
\State $Q \gets \op{RMSNorm}(Q)$ \Comment{normalise each head vector (stabilises training)}
\State $Q, K \gets \op{RoPE}(Q, K)$ \Comment{encode position by rotating vector pairs}
\State $P \gets \op{softmax}(s\, Q K^{\top} + M)$ \Comment{mask: causal + only the last 512 tokens}
\State $O \gets P V$ \Comment{$B{\times}T{\times}8{\times}256$}
\State $m \gets O\, W_O^{\top}$ \Comment{concatenate heads, project back to $B{\times}T{\times}1536$}
\State $h \gets r + \op{RMSNorm}(m)$ \Comment{post-attention norm, then add residual}
\Statex \hspace{-1.2em}\textit{channel mixing: feed-forward network (each token independently)}
\State $r \gets h;\ u \gets \op{RMSNorm}(h)$
\State $m \gets \big(\op{GELU}_{\tanh}(u W_{\mathrm{gate}}^{\top}) \odot u W_{\mathrm{up}}^{\top}\big) W_{\mathrm{down}}^{\top}$ \Comment{expand to 12288, gate, shrink back to 1536}
\State $h \gets r + \op{RMSNorm}(m)$ \Comment{post-feed-forward norm, then add residual}
\Statex \hspace{-1.2em}\textit{per-layer embedding injection}
\State $h \gets h + \op{RMSNorm}\big((\op{GELU}_{\tanh}(h W^{\mathrm{ple}\top}_{\mathrm{gate}}) \odot p_\ell) W^{\mathrm{ple}\top}_{\mathrm{proj}}\big)$ \Comment{gate down to 256, multiply by $p_\ell$, project back up}
\State $h \gets \gamma_\ell\, h$ \Comment{fixed per-layer scale}
\State \Return $h$
\end{algorithmic}
\end{algorithm}
\begin{algorithm}[H]
\caption{Block $\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{F2}}(h)$ --- Full (global) attention block (KV-shared, MLP width 12288)}
\begin{algorithmic}[1]
\Require $h$ of shape $B\times T\times 1536$, per-layer input $p_\ell$
\State $r \gets h$ \Comment{keep the input for the residual (skip) connection}
\State $u \gets \op{RMSNorm}(h)$ \Comment{normalise every token vector}
\Statex \hspace{-1.2em}\textit{token mixing: multi-head self-attention}
\State $Q \gets u W_Q^{\top}$ \Comment{$B{\times}T{\times}8{\times}512$ (8 query heads)}
\State $K, V \gets K^{(\ell^*)}, V^{(\ell^*)}$ \Comment{re-use keys/values of an earlier layer (saves compute and KV-cache)}
\State $Q \gets \op{RMSNorm}(Q)$ \Comment{normalise each head vector (stabilises training)}
\State $Q, K \gets \op{RoPE}(Q, K)$ \Comment{encode position by rotating vector pairs}
\State $P \gets \op{softmax}(s\, Q K^{\top} + M)$ \Comment{mask: causal: a token sees only earlier tokens}
\State $O \gets P V$ \Comment{$B{\times}T{\times}8{\times}512$}
\State $m \gets O\, W_O^{\top}$ \Comment{concatenate heads, project back to $B{\times}T{\times}1536$}
\State $h \gets r + \op{RMSNorm}(m)$ \Comment{post-attention norm, then add residual}
\Statex \hspace{-1.2em}\textit{channel mixing: feed-forward network (each token independently)}
\State $r \gets h;\ u \gets \op{RMSNorm}(h)$
\State $m \gets \big(\op{GELU}_{\tanh}(u W_{\mathrm{gate}}^{\top}) \odot u W_{\mathrm{up}}^{\top}\big) W_{\mathrm{down}}^{\top}$ \Comment{expand to 12288, gate, shrink back to 1536}
\State $h \gets r + \op{RMSNorm}(m)$ \Comment{post-feed-forward norm, then add residual}
\Statex \hspace{-1.2em}\textit{per-layer embedding injection}
\State $h \gets h + \op{RMSNorm}\big((\op{GELU}_{\tanh}(h W^{\mathrm{ple}\top}_{\mathrm{gate}}) \odot p_\ell) W^{\mathrm{ple}\top}_{\mathrm{proj}}\big)$ \Comment{gate down to 256, multiply by $p_\ell$, project back up}
\State $h \gets \gamma_\ell\, h$ \Comment{fixed per-layer scale}
\State \Return $h$
\end{algorithmic}
\end{algorithm}
\begin{algorithm}[H]
\caption{Block $\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{V}}(h)$ --- Vision encoder block}
\begin{algorithmic}[1]
\Require $h$ of shape $B\times T\times 768$
\State $r \gets h$ \Comment{keep the input for the residual (skip) connection}
\State $u \gets \op{RMSNorm}(h)$ \Comment{normalise every token vector}
\Statex \hspace{-1.2em}\textit{token mixing: multi-head self-attention}
\State $Q \gets u W_Q^{\top}$ \Comment{$B{\times}T{\times}12{\times}64$ (12 query heads)}
\State $K \gets u W_K^{\top};\ V \gets u W_V^{\top}$ \Comment{$B{\times}T{\times}12{\times}64$ (12 key/value heads)}
\State $Q \gets \op{RMSNorm}(Q);\ K \gets \op{RMSNorm}(K)$ \Comment{normalise each head vector (stabilises training)}
\State $V \gets \op{RMSNorm}(V)$
\State $Q, K \gets \op{RoPE}(Q, K)$ \Comment{2-D rotary position (row, column)}
\State $P \gets \op{softmax}(s\, Q K^{\top} + M)$ \Comment{mask: every patch/frame sees every other}
\State $O \gets P V$ \Comment{$B{\times}T{\times}12{\times}64$}
\State $m \gets O\, W_O^{\top}$ \Comment{concatenate heads, project back to $B{\times}T{\times}768$}
\State $h \gets r + \op{RMSNorm}(m)$ \Comment{post-attention norm, then add residual}
\Statex \hspace{-1.2em}\textit{channel mixing: feed-forward network (each token independently)}
\State $r \gets h;\ u \gets \op{RMSNorm}(h)$
\State $m \gets \big(\op{GELU}_{\tanh}(u W_{\mathrm{gate}}^{\top}) \odot u W_{\mathrm{up}}^{\top}\big) W_{\mathrm{down}}^{\top}$ \Comment{expand to 3072, gate, shrink back to 768}
\State $h \gets r + \op{RMSNorm}(m)$ \Comment{post-feed-forward norm, then add residual}
\State \Return $h$
\end{algorithmic}
\end{algorithm}
\begin{algorithm}[H]
\caption{Block $\mathcal{B}_{\mathrm{A}}(h)$ --- Conformer audio block}
\begin{algorithmic}[1]
\Require $h$ of shape $B\times T\times 1024$
\State $h \gets h + \tfrac12\,\op{FFN}_1(h)$ \Comment{first half-step feed-forward}
\State $h \gets h + \op{RMSNorm}(\op{Attn}_{rel}(\op{RMSNorm}(h)))$ \Comment{local self-attention over audio frames}
\State $h \gets h + \op{ConvModule}(h)$ \Comment{GLU $\to$ depthwise conv $\to$ norm $\to$ SiLU $\to$ linear}
\State $h \gets h + \tfrac12\,\op{FFN}_2(h)$ \Comment{second half-step feed-forward}
\State $h \gets \op{RMSNorm}(h)$
\State \Return $h$
\end{algorithmic}
\end{algorithm}

\section{What Each Layer Does}\label{sec:gloss}
One row per \emph{kind} of layer present in this model. ``Appears as'' gives the module names (layer indices removed).
\begin{longtable}{>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{5.4cm}>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{10.8cm}}
\toprule
Layer (appears as) & What it does \\
\midrule
\endfirsthead
\toprule
Layer (appears as) & What it does \\
\midrule
\endhead
\midrule \multicolumn{2}{r}{\textit{continued on next page}} \\
\endfoot
\bottomrule
\endlastfoot
\multicolumn{2}{l}{\textsc{Language model}} \\
\textbf{Token embedding}\newline{\footnotesize \texttt{embed\_tokens}} & Lookup table with one learned vector per vocabulary token. Turns token ids into the vectors the network computes with (Gemma multiplies them by $\sqrt{d}$). \\
\textbf{Per-layer token embedding (PLE)}\newline{\footnotesize \texttt{embed\_tokens\_per\_layer}} & A second embedding table looked up from the same token ids. It holds one small vector per token for every decoder layer, which gives each layer extra token-specific information cheaply. It can sit in CPU/flash memory, which is what the ``E'' (effective size) in the model name refers to. \\
\textbf{PLE gate (down-projection)}\newline{\footnotesize \texttt{layers.\allowbreak{}per\_layer\_input\_gate}} & Inside each layer: projects the hidden state down to the PLE size; after GELU it multiplies this layer's per-layer embedding $p_\ell$. \\
\textbf{PLE up-projection}\newline{\footnotesize \texttt{layers.\allowbreak{}per\_layer\_projection}} & Projects the gated per-layer signal back up to the hidden size before it is added to the residual stream. \\
\textbf{PLE output norm}\newline{\footnotesize \texttt{layers.\allowbreak{}post\_per\_layer\_input\_norm}} & RMSNorm on the per-layer contribution before the residual add. \\
\textbf{PLE context projection}\newline{\footnotesize \texttt{per\_layer\_model\_projection}} & Projects the main token embedding into one small vector per layer. Added to the PLE lookup, so the per-layer input depends on context as well as token identity. \\
\textbf{PLE projection norm}\newline{\footnotesize \texttt{per\_layer\_projection\_norm}} & RMSNorm on the projected per-layer inputs. \\
\textbf{Query projection (q\_proj)}\newline{\footnotesize \texttt{self\_attn.\allowbreak{}q\_proj}} & Turns each token vector into query vectors, one per head: ``what am I looking for?''. \\
\textbf{Query norm (q\_norm)}\newline{\footnotesize \texttt{self\_attn.\allowbreak{}q\_norm}} & RMSNorm applied to every query head vector before RoPE. Keeps attention logits in a sane range (QK-norm). \\
\textbf{Key norm (k\_norm)}\newline{\footnotesize \texttt{self\_attn.\allowbreak{}k\_norm}} & RMSNorm applied to every key head vector before RoPE. \\
\textbf{Key projection (k\_proj)}\newline{\footnotesize \texttt{self\_attn.\allowbreak{}k\_proj}} & Turns each token into key vectors: ``what do I contain?''. Queries are compared with keys to decide where to attend. With grouped-query attention there are fewer key heads than query heads. \\
\textbf{Value projection (v\_proj)}\newline{\footnotesize \texttt{self\_attn.\allowbreak{}v\_proj}} & Turns each token into value vectors: the information actually copied to tokens that attend to it. \\
\textbf{Output projection (o\_proj)}\newline{\footnotesize \texttt{self\_attn.\allowbreak{}o\_proj}} & Concatenates all heads' results and mixes them back into one hidden-size vector per token. \\
\textbf{Gate projection (gate\_proj)}\newline{\footnotesize \texttt{mlp.\allowbreak{}gate\_proj}} & Expands the token vector to the MLP width and passes it through SiLU/GELU. The result acts as a soft on/off switch for each hidden unit. \\
\textbf{Up projection (up\_proj)}\newline{\footnotesize \texttt{mlp.\allowbreak{}up\_proj}} & Expands the token vector to the MLP width (the ``content'' path). It is multiplied element-wise by the gate (SwiGLU/GeGLU). \\
\textbf{Down projection (down\_proj)}\newline{\footnotesize \texttt{mlp.\allowbreak{}down\_proj}} & Shrinks the gated hidden vector back to the model width so it can be added to the residual stream. \\
\textbf{Input norm (input\_layernorm)}\newline{\footnotesize \texttt{layers.\allowbreak{}input\_layernorm}} & RMSNorm applied to the residual stream before attention. Rescales each token vector to unit RMS, then multiplies by a learned per-channel weight. \\
\textbf{post\_attention\_layernorm}\newline{\footnotesize \texttt{layers.\allowbreak{}post\_attention\_layernorm}} & RMSNorm applied to the attention output before it is added to the residual stream (Gemma ``sandwich'' norm). \\
\textbf{Pre-MLP norm (pre\_feedforward\_layernorm)}\newline{\footnotesize \texttt{layers.\allowbreak{}pre\_feedforward\_layernorm}} & RMSNorm on the residual stream before the MLP (Gemma). \\
\textbf{Post-MLP norm (post\_feedforward\_layernorm)}\newline{\footnotesize \texttt{layers.\allowbreak{}post\_feedforward\_layernorm}} & RMSNorm on the MLP output before it is added back (Gemma ``sandwich'' norm). \\
\textbf{Final norm}\newline{\footnotesize \texttt{norm}} & RMSNorm applied once after the last decoder layer, just before the LM head. \\
\midrule \multicolumn{2}{l}{\textsc{Vision encoder}} \\
\textbf{2-D position table}\newline{\footnotesize \texttt{vision\_tower.\allowbreak{}patch\_embedder}} & Learned vectors for a patch's x and y coordinate, added so the encoder knows where each patch sits in the image. \\
\textbf{Patch embedding}\newline{\footnotesize \texttt{patch\_embedder.\allowbreak{}input\_proj}} & Each image patch (16$\times$16 pixels $\times$ 3 colours, flattened) is mapped linearly to one vector. \\
\textbf{Query projection (q\_proj)}\newline{\footnotesize \texttt{self\_attn.\allowbreak{}q\_proj}} & Turns each token vector into query vectors, one per head: ``what am I looking for?''. \\
\textbf{Key projection (k\_proj)}\newline{\footnotesize \texttt{self\_attn.\allowbreak{}k\_proj}} & Turns each token into key vectors: ``what do I contain?''. Queries are compared with keys to decide where to attend. With grouped-query attention there are fewer key heads than query heads. \\
\textbf{Value projection (v\_proj)}\newline{\footnotesize \texttt{self\_attn.\allowbreak{}v\_proj}} & Turns each token into value vectors: the information actually copied to tokens that attend to it. \\
\textbf{Output projection (o\_proj)}\newline{\footnotesize \texttt{self\_attn.\allowbreak{}o\_proj}} & Concatenates all heads' results and mixes them back into one hidden-size vector per token. \\
\textbf{Query norm (q\_norm)}\newline{\footnotesize \texttt{self\_attn.\allowbreak{}q\_norm}} & RMSNorm applied to every query head vector before RoPE. Keeps attention logits in a sane range (QK-norm). \\
\textbf{Key norm (k\_norm)}\newline{\footnotesize \texttt{self\_attn.\allowbreak{}k\_norm}} & RMSNorm applied to every key head vector before RoPE. \\
\textbf{Gate projection (gate\_proj)}\newline{\footnotesize \texttt{mlp.\allowbreak{}gate\_proj}} & Expands the token vector to the MLP width and passes it through SiLU/GELU. The result acts as a soft on/off switch for each hidden unit. \\
\textbf{Up projection (up\_proj)}\newline{\footnotesize \texttt{mlp.\allowbreak{}up\_proj}} & Expands the token vector to the MLP width (the ``content'' path). It is multiplied element-wise by the gate (SwiGLU/GeGLU). \\
\textbf{Down projection (down\_proj)}\newline{\footnotesize \texttt{mlp.\allowbreak{}down\_proj}} & Shrinks the gated hidden vector back to the model width so it can be added to the residual stream. \\
\textbf{Input norm (input\_layernorm)}\newline{\footnotesize \texttt{layers.\allowbreak{}input\_layernorm}} & RMSNorm applied to the residual stream before attention. Rescales each token vector to unit RMS, then multiplies by a learned per-channel weight. \\
\textbf{post\_attention\_layernorm}\newline{\footnotesize \texttt{layers.\allowbreak{}post\_attention\_layernorm}} & RMSNorm applied to the attention output before it is added to the residual stream (Gemma ``sandwich'' norm). \\
\textbf{Pre-MLP norm (pre\_feedforward\_layernorm)}\newline{\footnotesize \texttt{layers.\allowbreak{}pre\_feedforward\_layernorm}} & RMSNorm on the residual stream before the MLP (Gemma). \\
\textbf{Post-MLP norm (post\_feedforward\_layernorm)}\newline{\footnotesize \texttt{layers.\allowbreak{}post\_feedforward\_layernorm}} & RMSNorm on the MLP output before it is added back (Gemma ``sandwich'' norm). \\
\midrule \multicolumn{2}{l}{\textsc{Audio encoder}} \\
\textbf{Subsampling convolution}\newline{\footnotesize \texttt{layer0.\allowbreak{}conv}, \texttt{layer1.\allowbreak{}conv}} & 2-D convolution (stride 2) over the time $\times$ mel-frequency spectrogram. Two of them shorten the time axis 4$\times$, so the encoder works on fewer, richer frames. \\
\textbf{Subsampling norm}\newline{\footnotesize \texttt{layer0.\allowbreak{}norm}, \texttt{layer1.\allowbreak{}norm}} & LayerNorm over channels after each subsampling convolution. \\
\textbf{Audio input projection}\newline{\footnotesize \texttt{subsample\_conv\_projection.\allowbreak{}input\_proj\_linear}} & Flattens each frame's conv features and projects them to the audio-encoder width. \\
\textbf{Conformer FFN up-projection}\newline{\footnotesize \texttt{feed\_forward1.\allowbreak{}ffw\_layer\_1}, \texttt{feed\_forward2.\allowbreak{}ffw\_layer\_1}} & Expands each frame vector (e.g. 1024$\to$4096) before the SiLU non-linearity. \\
\textbf{Conformer FFN down-projection}\newline{\footnotesize \texttt{feed\_forward1.\allowbreak{}ffw\_layer\_2}, \texttt{feed\_forward2.\allowbreak{}ffw\_layer\_2}} & Projects back to encoder width. The result is halved (``macaron'' half-step) and added to the residual. \\
\textbf{Conformer FFN pre-norm}\newline{\footnotesize \texttt{feed\_forward1.\allowbreak{}pre\_layer\_norm}, \texttt{feed\_forward2.\allowbreak{}pre\_layer\_norm}} & RMSNorm before each of the two conformer feed-forward modules. \\
\textbf{Conformer FFN post-norm}\newline{\footnotesize \texttt{feed\_forward1.\allowbreak{}post\_layer\_norm}, \texttt{feed\_forward2.\allowbreak{}post\_layer\_norm}} & RMSNorm on the feed-forward output before the residual add. \\
\textbf{Per-dimension query scale}\newline{\footnotesize \texttt{layers.\allowbreak{}self\_attn}} & A learned scale per head dimension of the query, replacing the fixed $1/\sqrt{d_h}$. \\
\textbf{Audio Q/K/V projections}\newline{\footnotesize \texttt{self\_attn.\allowbreak{}q\_proj}, \texttt{self\_attn.\allowbreak{}k\_proj}, \texttt{self\_attn.\allowbreak{}v\_proj}} & Turn each audio frame into a query (what am I looking for), key (what do I contain) and value (what do I pass on). ``Clippable'' linears clamp activations for numerical safety. \\
\textbf{Audio attention output projection}\newline{\footnotesize \texttt{self\_attn.\allowbreak{}post}} & Mixes the concatenated head outputs back to encoder width. \\
\textbf{Relative-position key projection}\newline{\footnotesize \texttt{self\_attn.\allowbreak{}relative\_k\_proj}} & Projects relative-distance encodings so attention scores depend on how far apart two frames are (Transformer-XL style) instead of RoPE. \\
\textbf{Conv-module input projection (GLU)}\newline{\footnotesize \texttt{lconv1d.\allowbreak{}linear\_start}} & Doubles the width; a Gated Linear Unit then uses one half of the channels to gate the other half. \\
\textbf{Conv-module output projection}\newline{\footnotesize \texttt{lconv1d.\allowbreak{}linear\_end}} & Projects the convolved features back before the residual add. \\
\textbf{Depthwise causal 1-D convolution}\newline{\footnotesize \texttt{lconv1d.\allowbreak{}depthwise\_conv1d}} & Each channel is convolved over time with its own small kernel (5 frames), picking up local acoustic patterns such as phoneme transitions. \\
\textbf{Conv-module pre-norm}\newline{\footnotesize \texttt{lconv1d.\allowbreak{}pre\_layer\_norm}} & RMSNorm before the light-weight convolution module. \\
\textbf{Conv-module norm}\newline{\footnotesize \texttt{lconv1d.\allowbreak{}conv\_norm}} & RMSNorm after the depthwise convolution. \\
\textbf{Pre-attention norm}\newline{\footnotesize \texttt{layers.\allowbreak{}norm\_pre\_attn}} & RMSNorm applied to frames before audio self-attention. \\
\textbf{Post-attention norm}\newline{\footnotesize \texttt{layers.\allowbreak{}norm\_post\_attn}} & RMSNorm on the attention output before the residual add. \\
\textbf{Block output norm}\newline{\footnotesize \texttt{layers.\allowbreak{}norm\_out}} & RMSNorm at the end of every conformer block. \\
\textbf{Audio output projection}\newline{\footnotesize \texttt{audio\_tower.\allowbreak{}output\_proj}} & Maps the last audio-encoder states to the size the audio embedder expects. \\
\midrule \multicolumn{2}{l}{\textsc{Multimodal projector}} \\
\textbf{Image-to-text projector}\newline{\footnotesize \texttt{embed\_vision.\allowbreak{}embedding\_projection}} & Normalises pooled vision features and projects them into the language model's hidden size (``soft image tokens''). \\
\textbf{Audio-to-text projector}\newline{\footnotesize \texttt{embed\_audio.\allowbreak{}embedding\_projection}} & Normalises audio-encoder outputs and projects them into the language model's hidden size, so they can take the place of text-token embeddings. \\
\end{longtable}
